## Shared source texts

### T05fd9a0a3d254605c0ec69e8

Text hash 363a385bf2457bd6f7dd2c24b3ed7e8f79351cfed9507f00e319e2c3f5e5b376; absolute characters 83–632; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-app-executive-orders-6107-aa2111c449.txt

```text
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of part II of the act entitled "AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Government for the fiscal year ending June 30, 1933, and for other purposes", approved June 30, 1932, Reuben Perry, superintendent of the Albuquerque School, New Mexico, Indian Service, Department of the Interior, who during the current month reaches the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until June 30, 1933.
```

### T06894b187f664cc7724caa70

Text hash d40de2500e443af711a82a981c74da5b9ea0b5db2a6c8c518d07ca7bb0b2b810; absolute characters 0–606; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6874-amendment-subdivision-vii-schedule-the-civil-service-rules.txt

```text
By virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the provisions of the last sentence of the eighth paragraph of subdivision second of section 2 of the Civil Service Act of January 16, 1883 (ch. 27, 22 Stat. 403, 404), paragraph 6 of subdivision VII of schedule A of the civil-service rules is hereby amended to read as follows:

"6. Temporary clerks, earners, and laborers required for part-time or intermittent work in the Postal Service in connection with the present holiday or seasonal business from this date to February 1, 1935."

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 16, 1934.

```

### T2a6125cff8c32db3c07a73bd

Text hash 1f67a2dd2498bf11ce4a6ff233ff9146d2a8ae0c32f455261933f95962a03d1d; absolute characters 0–709; files: jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_4782.txt

```text
)xecuttve Orber
It is hereby ordered that the several executive departments and independent
Government establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government
Printing Office and the Navy Yard and Stations, be closed on Saturday, December
24, 1927, and all clerks and other employees in the Federal service in the District
of Columbia, except those who may for special public reasons be excepted from the
provisions of this Order, or those whose absence from duty would be inconsistent
with the provisions of existing law, are hereby excused from duty on that day.
This Order is not to be deemed as establishing a precedent.
CALVIN COOLIDGE
THE WHITE HOUSE,
December 16, 1927.
[No. 47821
Trlu
97f
```

### T2c7baa34be2e41ab71929166

```text
By virtue of the authority vested in me by section 204 of part II of the act entitled "AN ACT Making appropriations for the Legislative Branch of the Government for the fiscal year ending June 30, 1933, and for other purposes", approved June 30, 1932, Reuben Perry, superintendent of the Albuquerque School, New Mexico, Indian Service, Department of the Interior, who during the current month reaches the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until June 30, 1933.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

April 11, 1933.

```

G. Perry, Chief of the Division of Photolithography, at Manila, Philippine Islands, in the Coast and Geodetic Survey, Department of Commerce, who, during the current month, has reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, is hereby exempted from the provisions of that section until May 31, 1935.

```text
May 26, 1933.
```

### T4312025e1e2ef472e13d8f66

Text hash 7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666; absolute characters 0–1314; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * ";

And Whereas the public interest requires that Nelson H. Darton, senior scientist (geologist), Geological Survey, Department of the Interior, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until January 1, 1937:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt Nelson H. Darton from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until January 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 10, 1935.

```

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, ch. 314, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 692b), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * * ";

And Whereas the public interest requires that George G. Hedgcock, senior pathologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Department of Agriculture, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of two years by Executive Orders No. 6338 of October 14, 1933, and No. 6862 of September 30, 1934, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until November 1, 1936:

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

October 19, 1935.
```

### Tbfbe72a9636196f5890fc29e

Text hash 70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7; absolute characters 0–831; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt

```text
As an evidence of appreciation of the splendid service of the employees of the Government, most of whom have been working under exceptional pressure for the last 9½ months, it is hereby ordered that the several executive departments and independent Government establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government Printing Office and the Navy Yard and stations, be closed on Saturday, December 23, 1933, and Saturday, December 30, 1933, and all clerks and other employees in the Federal service in the District of Columbia, except those who may for special public reasons be excepted from the provisions of this order, or those whose absence from duty would be inconsistent with the provisions of existing law, are hereby excused from duty on those days.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

December 20, 1933.

```

```text
And Whereas the public interest requires that William H. Ramsey, senior attorney, Department of Justice, who, during the current month, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until February 1, 1937:

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William H. Ramsey from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until February 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 23, 1936.

```

### Tf419d7590cda5164d5058e93

Text hash a91cc501718893daf5ab4719f9b2398c71d4bc95f6a0f9fb9fae7e73382ff07b; absolute characters 0–1309; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7438-exemption-george-s-rice-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (5 U.S.C., sec. 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision of law or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that George S. Rice, chief mining engineer, Bureau of Mines, Department of the Interior, who, during the month of September, 1936, will reach the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, be exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until October 1, 1937;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt George S. Rice from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until October 1, 1937.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

August 21, 1936.

```

### Tf73ab78bf1cd0485b84db617

Text hash 21e7d1dfe74f5dc335785dbd4805a550b855c5aa32fe3202c731899d55228e7a; absolute characters 0–1314; files: jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7545-exemption-william-h-ramsey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt

```text
Whereas section 204 of the act of June 30, 1932, 47 Stat. 382, 404 (U.S. Code, Title 5, section 715a), provides:

"On and after July 1, 1932, no person rendering civilian service in any branch or service of the United States Government or the municipal government of the District of Columbia who shall have reached the retirement age prescribed for automatic separation from the service, applicable to such person, shall be continued in such service, notwithstanding any provision ofjaw or regulation to the contrary: Provided, That the President may, by Executive Order, exempt from the provisions of this section any person when, in his judgment, the public interest so requires * * *";

And Whereas the public interest requires that William H. Ramsey, Attorney, Criminal Division, Department of Justice, who was exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7278 of January 23, 1936, be further exempted from the provisions of this section and continued in the service until February 1, 1938;

Now, Therefore, by virtue of and pursuant to the authority vested in me by the aforesaid section, I do hereby exempt William H. Ramsey from the provisions thereof and continue him in the service until February 1, 1938.

FRANKLIN D. ROOSEVELT

The White House,

January 29, 1937.

```

The OCR layer is complete; its errors ('I19' for 19, a stray apostrophe after 'hereby', '[No. 1547-.1') do not affect the operative words. Single operative command: under section 2 of the act of June 19, 1912 limiting the hours of daily service of laborers and mechanics on federal work (the 1912 eight-hour act), the act's provisions and stipulations 'are hereby waived, until January first, nineteen hundred and fifteen, as to any contract or contracts entered into in connection with the construction of the Isthmian Canal.' First end: by its own terms on 1915-01-01. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the 'until' date is inclusive, so the waiver runs through the end of January 1, 1915; the alternative exclusive reading is that it stopped when that day began, leaving December 31, 1914 as its last day. Authority check: no held source shows section 2 of the 1912 act repealed or amended before 1915; the held text of EO 2663 (1917, jobs/wikisource-texts/output/texts/EO_2663.txt) invokes section 2 of the same act (37 Stat. 137), which corroborates that the authority still existed after this order's end. The act's own effective-date clause is not held; it does not affect the end. The order states its own date, so its end does not depend on when the canal construction contracts were completed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end is held. Candidates considered and not shown to extinguish the waiver: the Panama Canal Act of August 24, 1912 and EO 1885 (January 27, 1914, permanent organization of the Panama Canal) with EO 1888 (February 2, 1914, conditions of employment). The waiver attaches to contracts for canal construction, not to the Isthmian Canal Commission, and neither order's held summary or text names EO 1547 or the 1912 act's waiver; under the owner's rule a later general instrument ends a command only if something shows it extinguished that command, and nothing held does. The Panama Canal Act's text is not held. No extension: census titles, the WPA 1944 numbered list and the full text of all 2,750 ProQuest scan texts were searched on 2026-10-28 for Isthmian or Panama Canal orders touching eight-hour or hours-of-labor rules between 1912 and 1916; the only hits were EO 1547 itself, EO 2514 (January 15, 1917, Canal Zone leave rules, effective December 31, 1916) and EO 4459 (1926, Canal overtime pay), none of which names this waiver or acts before 1915. A citation search of every held order text for 'No. 1547' or 'Order 1547' found only Proclamation No. 1547 of 1919 (Scotts Bluff), a different instrument. The date is a fixed term of the waiver, not a deadline on another act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No independent command survives. The recital of section 2 of the 1912 act is authority, not a command. The waiver has no proviso imposing a separate continuing duty.\",\"savings_check\":\"Contracts entered into while the waiver ran could lawfully omit the eight-hour stipulations, and work done under them was lawful. Pay for hours worked flows from the contracts and statute, not from the order. No held source shows any contract affected after 1915; this residual is a disclosed qualification, not a finding.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole order is one clause: a waiver for one class of contracts with one stated term. No partial amendment, extension or revocation of any part is held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:1547#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"}]},{"claim_sha256":"f8fb155600b1d7ee48ea7f750f4d3af482d58c4f10276680aeb523a927660218","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"e2ac1fabf9eee1f2168da47c8d29e7355401d6d9c27b1feb8d04577bdda0be9a","path":"research/excavator/claude-spent-probe-2026-10-28/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:1"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"EO 1547 (June 19, 1912, William H. Taft), read in full in the held ProQuest replica scan (archival_replica_scan_pending_codebook, acceptable_as_original false) and checked against the page image I rendered from the held PDF (sha256 2e446a81...; one page; printed number 'No. 1547', date 'June 19, 1912', signature 'Wm H Taft'). The OCR layer is complete; its errors ('I19' for 19, a stray apostrophe after 'hereby', '[No. 1547-.1') do not affect the operative words. Single operative command: under section 2 of the act of June 19, 1912 limiting the hours of daily service of laborers and mechanics on federal work (the 1912 eight-hour act), the act's provisions and stipulations 'are hereby waived, until January first, nineteen hundred and fifteen, as to any contract or contracts entered into in connection with the construction of the Isthmian Canal.' First end: by its own terms on 1915-01-01. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the 'until' date is inclusive, so the waiver runs through the end of January 1, 1915; the alternative exclusive reading is that it stopped when that day began, leaving December 31, 1914 as its last day. Authority check: no held source shows section 2 of the 1912 act repealed or amended before 1915; the held text of EO 2663 (1917, jobs/wikisource-texts/output/texts/EO_2663.txt) invokes section 2 of the same act (37 Stat. 137), which corroborates that the authority still existed after this order's end. The act's own effective-date clause is not held; it does not affect the end. The order states its own date, so its end does not depend on when the canal construction contracts were completed.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end is held. Candidates considered and not shown to extinguish the waiver: the Panama Canal Act of August 24, 1912 and EO 1885 (January 27, 1914, permanent organization of the Panama Canal) with EO 1888 (February 2, 1914, conditions of employment). The waiver attaches to contracts for canal construction, not to the Isthmian Canal Commission, and neither order's held summary or text names EO 1547 or the 1912 act's waiver; under the owner's rule a later general instrument ends a command only if something shows it extinguished that command, and nothing held does. The Panama Canal Act's text is not held. No extension: census titles, the WPA 1944 numbered list and the full text of all 2,750 ProQuest scan texts were searched on 2026-10-28 for Isthmian or Panama Canal orders touching eight-hour or hours-of-labor rules between 1912 and 1916; the only hits were EO 1547 itself, EO 2514 (January 15, 1917, Canal Zone leave rules, effective December 31, 1916) and EO 4459 (1926, Canal overtime pay), none of which names this waiver or acts before 1915. A citation search of every held order text for 'No. 1547' or 'Order 1547' found only Proclamation No. 1547 of 1919 (Scotts Bluff), a different instrument. The date is a fixed term of the waiver, not a deadline on another act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No independent command survives. The recital of section 2 of the 1912 act is authority, not a command. The waiver has no proviso imposing a separate continuing duty.\",\"savings_check\":\"Contracts entered into while the waiver ran could lawfully omit the eight-hour stipulations, and work done under them was lawful. Where such a contract continued after January 1, 1915, its terms stood as made; that is an accrued effect of a contract formed under the waiver, carried by the contract and the 1912 act, and it does not keep the order operative, because the order commands nothing after its stated date. Pay for hours worked flows from the contracts and statute, not from the order. No held source shows any contract affected after 1915; this residual is a disclosed qualification, not a finding.\",\"scope_check\":\"Whole order is one clause: a waiver for one class of contracts with one stated term. No partial amendment, extension or revocation of any part is held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":[],"alignment":"scope_or_source_reconciliation_required","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:1547#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7","path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","sha256":"ba9127150a42a4ea81ddbc6e2b04ec70390e5d19e738088aaeb0707a4792115a","state":"changed"},"sha256":"2e446a8148b78c1398a3d3a90c3cdce3887444eadc3abe69942b84d67afb198e","text_path":"jobs/pinakes-proquest-ingest/output/texts/EO_1547.txt","text_sha256":"537c53c6d0a9cea85bd02b9d633aad5d1bdb78a390184c5ff5e1ce5d6f2e2bc7"}]}]}

Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6107 (April 11, 1933), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Reuben Perry, superintendent of the Albuquerque School, Indian Service, Interior from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until June 30, 1933. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1933-06-30, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6107 (April 11, 1933), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Reuben Perry, superintendent of the Albuquerque School, Indian Service, Interior from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until June 30, 1933. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1933-06-30, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6107#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:6107#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6107-exemption-reuben-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3","state":"match"},"sha256":"aa2111c4494f568c05ba7d2c1079b6b69dcec8abd9e37f014ea5d794be94934f","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6107-exemption-reuben-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6107-exemption-reuben-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3","state":"match"},"sha256":"aa2111c4494f568c05ba7d2c1079b6b69dcec8abd9e37f014ea5d794be94934f","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6107-exemption-reuben-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"cf8624d8a99545ff86edf44f9c23e1c3204b93c80a8038f3fad0421037e742c3"}]}]}

Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6146 (May 26, 1933), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Walter L. G. Perry, Chief of the Division of Photolithography at Manila, Coast and Geodetic Survey, Commerce from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until May 31, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1935-05-31, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"d08195612d68b10491addee01ee5e336dfce1aa7db1a2b21e73ade0d266841e7","path":"research/excavator/claude-blind-review-2026-10-28/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:3","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6146#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:6146#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6146-exemption-walter-l-g-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45","state":"match"},"sha256":"fd61532e84db4cd29179c0f4e0db7ec076b2b4447f0f2ecebec9cf549478e2aa","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6146-exemption-walter-l-g-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6146-exemption-walter-l-g-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45","state":"match"},"sha256":"fd61532e84db4cd29179c0f4e0db7ec076b2b4447f0f2ecebec9cf549478e2aa","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6146-exemption-walter-l-g-perry-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"64b74fa512c2b21248eebcda17f42d6e1ac6dbf09dd5c7376369a9c583949c45"}]}]}

It closes the executive departments and independent establishments in the District of Columbia, including the Government Printing Office and the Navy Yard and stations, on Saturday, December 23, 1933 and Saturday, December 30, 1933, and excuses federal employees there from duty on those days, except those excepted for special public reasons or whose absence would conflict with existing law. The recital of appreciation for the employees' service is not a command. First end: by its own terms at the close of 1933-12-30, the second and last day it governs (the December 23 part was spent at the close of that day). Searches: census titles and type rows, the WPA 1944 list (1933 closings) and a full-text citation search found no later order naming EO 6516; the census hit EO 6559 (1934) concerns banks and a different proclamation of December 30, 1933.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end: nothing held amends or revokes the order before December 23, 1933; each closing day ends at its close by the order's own terms. The two days are one command with a fixed period, not a deadline.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The closing and excusal are one command for two stated days; the recital of appreciation commands no one.\",\"savings_check\":\"None stated; pay for the excused days follows statute and does not keep the order operative.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause covering two stated days; no partial event held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:6516#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:6516#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt","sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7","state":"match"},"sha256":"ce41326769a06e9a12003bfe7165809f982aeb8236565204f2ae3d04ae1749ca","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt","text_sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt","sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7","state":"match"},"sha256":"ce41326769a06e9a12003bfe7165809f982aeb8236565204f2ae3d04ae1749ca","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-6516-excusing-federal-employees-the-district-columbia-from-duty-december.txt","text_sha256":"70d90d1421dee9ba6a699005b384b5a29eb2fdccdff709744a6cc9f72bddf2f7"}]}]}

Rejected versions and contradictory assertions remain visible; whole-case review is still required.","remaining_delta":{"current_reported_gaps":[],"dossier_fingerprint":"a580d35b8f8b06bc78b3840bae5981a376b9c789193dd64531ff162b2cad49d2","existing_lifecycle_questions":[{"clause_sha256":"9c2cc4459b63ea1c76665708c98754b3ff7604f193336d7e534e773fe01ba016","dossier_pointer":"/lifecycle_obligations/0","later_actor_evidence_ids":[],"own_term_evidence_ids":["TERM:EO:6663:e53c7b298734dc95@e8164f5e9405722d","TERM:EO:6663:cbd548ab3afebc41@e8164f5e9405722d","TERM:EO:6663:e53c7b298734dc95@ef2cec038288bfde","TERM:EO:6663:cbd548ab3afebc41@ef2cec038288bfde"],"questions":["Determine the first cessation or current predicate for this scope; check independent commands and surviving effects.","Confirm the relevant event or operative sunset, scope, extensions and renewals; a detected date remains a candidate."],"scope_id":"EO:6663#whole","state":"requires_clause_specific_research"}],"existing_lifecycle_questions_notice":"Current dossier research prompts, not new gap findings or overrides of prior claim/review conclusions.","new_legal_gaps_invented":[],"prior_differences_require_scope_source_proposition_review":true},"selection_and_checkpoint_records":[],"state":"prior_work_located","status_holds":[],"status_research":[],"unattached_status_reviews":[],"unfinished_work":[],"versions":[{"claim_sha256":"6e33a4ad152332423d2d000611080c0c26c8eac964e174c817dde88efca81c14","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"a20a0c2682b119eefc0074a1defd7500f2ae615589c5aeb51b42b8d0b4a225ce","path":"research/excavator/claude-spent-probe-2026-10-28/packet-v4-supp/applied-v1/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:4"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"Text and identity: read in full in the registered American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6663 (March 31, 1934), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Luther L. Browning, linotype machinist in charge, Government Printing Office from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until April 1, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders further exempting the same person are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 6984 (March 7, 1935) recites EO 6663 and further exempts Browning until April 1, 1936; EO 7307 (February 28, 1936) recites EO 6663 and EO 6984 and further exempts him until November 1, 1936. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. EO 7645 confirms the reading by counting the earlier orders as separate 'periods totaling three years and eight months'. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1936-11-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text. Legal reading: ends by its own terms on 1935-04-01, inclusive. The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this person after the last extension. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 6663 (March 31, 1934), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Luther L. Browning, linotype machinist in charge, Government Printing Office from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until April 1, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders further exempting the same person are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 6984 (March 7, 1935) recites EO 6663 and further exempts Browning until April 1, 1936; EO 7307 (February 28, 1936) recites EO 6663 and EO 6984 and further exempts him until November 1, 1936. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The held extension texts confirm the reading: EO 6984 recites Browning as 'exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of 1 year by Executive Order No. 6663', and EO 7307 recites him as exempted 'for a period of two years by Executive Orders No. 6663 ... and No. 6984', counting each earlier order as a completed period. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1936-11-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text. Legal reading: ends by its own terms on 1935-04-01, inclusive. The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this person after the last extension. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. EO 6825 (August 20, 1934), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts C. Frank Wittenauer, picture engraver, Bureau of Engraving and Printing from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until September 1, 1935. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1935-09-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Roosevelt), read in full. Text and identity: read in full in the registered American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy.  It is not a closing order (the type label closing_mourning is wrong; the WPA 1944 list, 1935 CSR 28, and the APP title agree): under the Civil Service Act of January 16, 1883 it amends paragraph 6 of subdivision VII of schedule A of the civil-service rules 'to read as follows': '6. Temporary clerks, carriers, and laborers required for part-time or intermittent work in the Postal Service in connection with the present holiday or seasonal business from this date to February 1, 1935.' Decision on its own terms. The order's one command is the rewrite of paragraph 6; under the strike-and-insert ruling the rewritten paragraph is the operative clause and continues, amended, until something ends it. Two candidate ends exist in held evidence. (a) The inserted text's own period: the paragraph excepts positions only 'from this date to February 1, 1935'. That period is part of the exception's definition, so after February 1, 1935 the paragraph excepted no position; the text itself is the evidence that its operation ended, and nothing held extended it (the WPA 1944 list shows no amendment of subdivision VII paragraph 6 between October 16, 1934 and October 19, 1935). (b) EO 7209 (October 19, 1935; held government-printed text, added here as SUPPLEMENT:EO7209) amends paragraph 6 'to read as follows' with a new period to February 1, 1936; its words show it replaced the paragraph's text, but by then the 1934 exception had already stopped operating. Conclusion: the clause terminated by its own terms on 1935-02-01, read inclusively (exclusive alternative: it stopped when that day began, so January 31, 1935 was its last day); EO 7209's replacement of the spent text on 1935-10-19 is recorded as a later event, not as the end. Under the evidence-decides ruling EO 7209 is not a default end, and here the earlier own-terms end is evidenced. Savings: appointments made under the exception during its period are accrued; they do not keep the exception operative. Earlier versions of paragraph 6 (EO 5731, 1931; EO 5930, 1932; EO 6352, 1933) precede this order and do not affect its end.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end: nothing held amended or revoked paragraph 6 between October 16, 1934 and February 1, 1935 (WPA 1944 list, all subdivision VII entries 1934-1935). EO 7209 (October 19, 1935) rewrote the already-spent paragraph and is recorded as a later event, not as the end, because the own-terms end is evidenced first (evidence-decides ruling, rule 3).\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has one command, the rewrite of paragraph 6; the recital of the Civil Service Act is authority.\",\"savings_check\":\"Appointments made under the exception during its period are accrued and do not keep it operative.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause: the rewrite of paragraph 6. Earlier versions of paragraph 6 (EO 5731, 5930, 6352) precede this order and are not part of its Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7208 (October 19, 1935), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts George G. Hedgcock, senior pathologist, Bureau of Plant Industry, Agriculture from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until November 1, 1936. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. The order recites EO 6338 (October 14, 1933) and EO 6862 (September 30, 1934), recited earlier exemptions. Later orders further exempting the same person are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 7475 (October 19, 1936) recites EO 6338, 6862 and 7208 and further exempts Hedgcock until July 1, 1937; EO 7645 (June 24, 1937) recites EO 6338, 6862, 7208 and 7475 (\"periods totaling three years and eight months\") and further exempts him until January 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. EO 7645 confirms the reading by counting the earlier orders as separate 'periods totaling three years and eight months'. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1938-01-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text. Legal reading: ends by its own terms on 1936-11-01, inclusive. The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this person after the last extension. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. EO 7645 confirms the reading by counting the earlier orders as separate 'periods totaling three years and eight months'. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1938-01-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date. Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7247 (December 10, 1935), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Nelson H. Darton, senior scientist (geologist), Geological Survey, Interior from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until January 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-01-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or and identity: read in full in the registered American Presidency Project transcription (jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts; grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7247 (December 10, 1935), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Nelson H. Darton, senior scientist (geologist), Geological Survey, Interior from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until January 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-01-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"9709a2f75a00c0fa81e7a509f2a5db1ac35af6945b22c75c072ea87d76b8fbb8","path":"research/excavator/claude-blind-review-2026-10-28/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:2","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld_after_reveal","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:7247#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:7247#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666","state":"match"},"sha256":"67cfd1025fc6b8ec50a39ec1ca2fd41ecd9a697996ed5bedb80291298f194b1e","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666","state":"match"},"sha256":"67cfd1025fc6b8ec50a39ec1ca2fd41ecd9a697996ed5bedb80291298f194b1e","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7247-exemption-nelson-h-darton-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"7e86066099a298d99a3602480346c73632461f08d1af5a1f304780c6216d9666"}]}]}

EO 7255 (December 21, 1935), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Elton A. Gongwer, investigator, General Accounting Office from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until January 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders further exempting the same person are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 7516 (December 16, 1936) recites EO 7255 and further exempts Gongwer until January 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. EO 7645 confirms the reading by counting the earlier orders as separate 'periods totaling three years and eight months'. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1938-01-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text. Legal reading: ends by its own terms on 1937-01-01, inclusive. The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this person after the last extension. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. EO 7278 (January 23, 1936), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts William H. Ramsey, senior attorney, Justice from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until February 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Later orders further exempting the same person are held and added to this packet as source-supplement excerpts: EO 7545 (January 29, 1937) recites EO 7278 and further exempts Ramsey until February 1, 1938. Each is recorded as a later event on the clause (clauses.csv). Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. The held extension text confirms the reading: EO 7545 recites Ramsey as 'exempted from compulsory retirement for a period of one year by Executive Order No. 7278', counting this order as a completed period. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1938-02-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text. Legal reading: ends by its own terms on 1937-02-01, inclusive. The census, WPA and full-text name searches found no further order naming this person after the last extension. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act. Extension reading, decided on the texts: each later order is a new exercise of the section 204 proviso with its own period. None says that the earlier order 'is amended' or alters its date; each recites the earlier exemption as a past grant ('who was exempted ... by Executive Order No. ...') and grants a further exemption 'until' a new date. EO 7645 confirms the reading by counting the earlier orders as separate 'periods totaling three years and eight months'. The strike-and-insert ruling does not apply because nothing is struck or inserted. So this order's own command ended on its own stated date, inclusive (exclusive alternative: ended when that day began), and each extension is recorded as a later event naming the same employee; the employee's exempt status continued without a gap under the extension orders. The alternative reading, that the extensions prolonged this order's command, would put its first end at the last extension's 'until' date (1938-02-01, inclusive); the texts do not support it, because no extension operates on this order's text.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; the later further-exemption orders are recorded as later events and do not amend this order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"d08195612d68b10491addee01ee5e336dfce1aa7db1a2b21e73ade0d266841e7","path":"research/excavator/claude-blind-review-2026-10-28/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:16","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:7278#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:7278#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"4b9e75e385a3f439f2dc1762263721b3b0af81c12daa9145ca68b97494f350be","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7278-exemption-william-h-ramsey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"4b9e75e385a3f439f2dc1762263721b3b0af81c12daa9145ca68b97494f350be","state":"match"},"sha256":"8bd68dd1a43732f95420adf2e64dead47967b2fc2067a65398c59dc1d0448c42","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7278-exemption-william-h-ramsey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"4b9e75e385a3f439f2dc1762263721b3b0af81c12daa9145ca68b97494f350be"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"21e7d1dfe74f5dc335785dbd4805a550b855c5aa32fe3202c731899d55228e7a","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7545-exemption-william-h-ramsey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"21e7d1dfe74f5dc335785dbd4805a550b855c5aa32fe3202c731899d55228e7a","state":"match"},"sha256":"e72a2be9f5a02a4bed0ed4cf13fd7f589f869a505cc46b52110c7110b2dc2e5c","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7545-exemption-william-h-ramsey-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"21e7d1dfe74f5dc335785dbd4805a550b855c5aa32fe3202c731899d55228e7a"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"21e7d1dfe74f5dc335785dbd4805a550b855c5aa32fe3202c731899d55228e7a","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7545-exemption-william-h-ramsey-fr grade mirror_transcription, acceptable_as_original false; standing note: original not yet held). Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7310 (February 28, 1936), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts W. H. Moran, Chief of the Secret Service Division, Treasury from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until January 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. The order recites EO 6647 of March 17, 1934 (recited earlier exemption; an earlier grant, not an event on this order). Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-01-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or found.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"d08195612d68b10491addee01ee5e336dfce1aa7db1a2b21e73ade0d266841e7","path":"research/excavator/claude-blind-review-2026-10-28/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:7310#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:7310#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7310-exemption-w-h-moran-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0","state":"match"},"sha256":"bafdd468963ebb069e80ce53bfd8cbf8d1eccc33dfb0a4e2eba464a3af4dac4d","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7310-exemption-w-h-moran-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0","path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7310-exemption-w-h-moran-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0","state":"match"},"sha256":"bafdd468963ebb069e80ce53bfd8cbf8d1eccc33dfb0a4e2eba464a3af4dac4d","text_path":"jobs/app-executive-orders/output/texts/executive-order-7310-exemption-w-h-moran-from-compulsory-retirement-for-age.txt","text_sha256":"120070c861a2c51274fa3a05405fb061efda47f215103a290b5df10590948fc0"}]},{"claim_sha256":"fcb209d4fc7849696db3e2046de8d9f2b91cb57d76e626f95958d5b72d4e0ac2","current_canonical":false,"locations":[{"file_sha256":"bf49a6ca76a831049bfa67d72ab65cdeb4d64fe42a94dc0fb01ab54266ddac56","path":"research/ex Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7394 (June 19, 1936), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Benjamin F. Taylor, Chief, Application Division, Copyright Office, Library of Congress from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until August 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. The order recites EO 7093 of July 8, 1935 (recited earlier exemption; an earlier grant, not an event on this order). Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-08-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or
