Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7401 (June 23, 1936), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts Henry Blackwood, Assistant Collector of Customs, Treasury from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until July 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-07-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no Page check per jobs/claim-kit/AGENT_PROTOCOL.md (working-text paragraph), done 2026-10-28: the APP page record with the same sha256 in jobs/app-executive-orders/output/(MIRROR_)DOCUMENTS.jsonl has a heading naming this order number, a date and subject agreeing with the held text, and a text_sha256 equal to the held file's. Clauses are quoted from that registered file, not from the unregistered clause-drafts copy. EO 7438 (August 21, 1936), read in full in the held American Presidency Project transcription (two held copies of the same page, sha256 shared), exempts George S. Rice, chief mining engineer, Bureau of Mines, Interior from section 204 of the Economy Act of June 30, 1932 and continues the employee in the service until October 1, 1937. Identity: number, date, person and title agree across the APP page, the census register and the WPA 1944 numbered list. Legal reading: a single time-limited grant that ends by its own terms on 1937-10-01, inclusive; the census, WPA and full-text name searches found no later order naming this person, so no extension or earlier end is held. Authority: section 204 of Part II of the Legislative Appropriation Act of June 30, 1932 (47 Stat. 382, 404), whose proviso lets the President exempt a person from automatic separation at retirement age. No held source shows section 204 repealed or amended before this order's stated end. Held texts of later exemptions show the President still granting exemptions under section 204 after this date (for example EO 7725 of October 1937, under section 204, running to November 1, 1938), which corroborates that the statutory scheme ran through the stated date.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms end on the stated 'until' date. Under the owner's 2026-10-21 ruling the end is recorded as that date, inclusive (the exemption runs through the end of that day); the exclusive reading, under which the exemption stops when that day begins, is the stated alternative. The employee's death or separation before the date would leave the exemption unused but is not, on the order's text, an end of the order, and no held record shows such an event. The date is a fixed term of the grant, not a deadline on some other act.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains one operative command, the time-limited exemption of one named employee. The recital of section 204 and the identification of the employee are not independent commands.\",\"savings_check\":\"Service performed and pay earned while the exemption ran are accrued effects. Any annuity or retirement-credit consequence flows from the retirement statutes, not from the order, and does not keep the order operative: the order commands nothing after its stated date and no later order names it.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, one named person; no partial event held or
